**Plan presented for approval:**

### Phase 2B: Customer and Commerce persistence, and synthetic data seeding

#### Context

Phase 2A delivered the Customer and Commerce schema and domain model, but nothing writes to
them yet. Phase 2B adds two things:

- the insert-only GORM persistence that seeding needs;
- a deterministic, idempotent seeder.

The seeder fills an empty database with about 5,000 synthetic customers in TR, DE and GB, with
their permissions, baskets and orders. Segmentation and campaigns then have realistic data to
work on.

Out of scope: Campaign, Segmentation, Delivery and AI. Basket history is also out of scope,
and its limitation gets documented as a TODO.

#### Decisions, and where I push back

| Topic | Decision | Alternative rejected |
|---|---|---|
| Where seeding runs | A new `cmd/seed` binary, run by a one-shot Compose service. Startup becomes postgres → seed → api → web, so the API starts with data | Seeding inside the API on startup puts demo-data code in the production binary and slows every start |
| Idempotency | One transaction that takes a PostgreSQL advisory lock, then skips if any customer exists. Reseed with `docker compose down -v` | Upserts on natural keys (orders have none); truncate-and-reseed (destroys data) |
| Determinism | A fixed random seed. Every timestamp is relative to the seeding time, which is passed in as `now`. The same seed and `now` always produce the identical dataset | Fixed absolute dates: the data would age, and "last 30 days" segments would empty out |
| Efficiency | GORM `CreateInBatches` in batches of 1,000 rows, inserted in dependency order with IDs returned. That is about 70k rows in a few seconds | COPY or parallel inserts: not needed at this size, and they bypass GORM |
| Realism | Hand-written lists of names, cities and products, plus six behaviour personas | A faker library: a new dependency with poor Turkish data, and random data doesn't guarantee segment coverage |
| Persistence | Insert-only `Store` types in `customer` and `commerce`, with unexported GORM row structs. The domain types stay unchanged, and the seeder owns the transaction | Full repositories with reads and updates: nothing calls them yet |
| Contact data | Emails use the reserved `example.com`, `example.net` and `example.org` domains (RFC 2606). Phone numbers are synthetic | Faker data that could match real people |

#### Dataset (5,000 customers by default)

##### Geography and language

| Country | Share | Cities | Languages |
|---|---|---|---|
| TR | 60% | 12 (İstanbul is about 35% of TR) | `tr` 92%, `en` 8% |
| DE | 25% | 8 | `de` 70%, `tr` 25% (the Turkish diaspora), `en` 5% |
| GB | 15% | 8 | `en` 90%, `tr` 10% |

Names follow the customer's language.

##### Contact details and permissions

- About 90% of customers have a phone: TR 95%, DE 80%, GB 85%.
- For each channel a customer can be reached on:
  - 85% were asked for permission.
  - Of those asked, 70% granted email and 55% granted SMS.
  - 10% of grants were later revoked.
- A customer who was never asked has no row, which means "not permitted".

##### Catalogue

8 categories × 3 brands × 7 items, about 170 products, priced in TRY (kuruş).

##### Personas

| Persona | Share | Signed up | Completed orders | Last order | Open basket |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| New | 8% | last 30 days | 0–1 | — | 50% |
| Browser | 17% | last 3 years | none | — | 45% |
| Occasional | 30% | last 3 years | 1–3 | any time | 20% |
| Regular | 20% | 6+ months ago | 4–9 | last 60 days | 25% |
| Loyal | 10% | 1+ year ago | 10–15 | last 14 days | 30% |
| Lapsed | 15% | 1+ year ago | 2–6 | 6–18 months ago | 15% |

- **Order lines:**
  - 1 to 4 distinct products per order, 70% of them from the customer's 1–2 favourite
    categories.
  - Quantities are mostly 1.
  - The unit price is the product's price.
- **Order status:**
  - 90% completed;
  - 6% cancelled, 1–3 days after the order;
  - 4% refunded, 7–30 days after the order, never in the future.
- **Open baskets:**
  - 1 to 4 lines, all added after the customer's last order.
  - 70% were added within the last 30 days (abandoned); the other 30% are older.
- **Expected volume:** about 6,500 permissions, 19,000 orders, 38,000 order lines and 3,000
  basket lines.

#### Segmentation scenarios the tests guarantee

Unit tests check every scenario in Go on the default dataset. One integration test also runs
scenario 1 as SQL.

1. **The Phase 2A example:** at least 25 customers in İstanbul who added to their basket in
   the last 30 days, have no completed order in the last 30 days, and allow SMS.
2. **Never purchased:** at least 15% of customers.
3. **Lapsed** (last completed order 180+ days ago): at least 10%.
4. **Loyal** (10+ completed orders): at least 5%.
5. **New** (signed up in the last 30 days): at least 5%.
6. **Geography and language:** every city has customers, and at least 15% of DE customers
   prefer `tr`.
7. **Order outcomes:** at least 3% of orders are cancelled, and at least 2% refunded.
8. **Contactability:** at least 5% of customers have no phone, and at least 2% revoked a
   permission.
9. **Category affinity:** each category has buyers within the last 90 days.

#### Changes

1. **Shared helpers** (small refactors of existing code):
   - `internal/platform/config`: a `Config.NewLogger(w)` method, moved from `cmd/api/main.go`
     so both binaries build their logger the same way.
   - `internal/platform/database/databasetest`: `New(t) *gorm.DB`. This is the
     throwaway-database helper from `migrations/migrations_test.go`, now shared by the
     integration tests of three packages.
2. **Insert-only persistence:**
   - `internal/customer/store.go`: `NewStore(*gorm.DB)`, `CreateCountries`, `CreateCities`,
     `CreateCustomers` (customers first, then their permissions; IDs are written back) and
     `HasCustomers`.
   - `internal/commerce/store.go`: `CreateCategories`, `CreateProducts`, `CreateOrders`
     (orders first, then their lines) and `CreateBaskets`.
   - Row structs are unexported and name their tables explicitly. GORM's automatic timestamps
     are turned off so generated times are stored as given, and the optional phone is a
     `*string`.
3. **The seeder, `internal/seed`:**
   - `catalog.go`: countries, cities with weights, categories, and brands × items with prices.
   - `people.go`: names per language, emails such as `[REDACTED EMAIL]` (names
     folded to ASCII), and unique synthetic phone numbers.
   - `generate.go`: customers with their personas and permissions, then orders and baskets per
     persona. Everything is built through the domain constructors, so every Phase 2A
     invariant holds.
   - `seed.go`: `Run(ctx, db, opts, now, logger) (Result, error)`. It works in one transaction:
     - take a `pg_advisory_xact_lock`;
     - skip if `HasCustomers` returns true;
     - insert reference data, products and customers, then orders and baskets, using the IDs
       returned by the earlier batches;
     - log counts and duration, with no personal data.

     Bulk inserts log at GORM's error level only. Slow-query warnings would otherwise print
     statements with thousands of placeholders.
4. **`cmd/seed/main.go`:**
   - Load the config, build the logger and call `database.Open`.
   - Call `database.Migrate`. It is idempotent and locked, so seeding works even before the
     API has ever run.
   - Call `seed.Run` with 5,000 customers, a fixed seed and `time.Now()`. Tests pass smaller
     counts through the options.
5. **Compose and Makefile:**
   - `compose.yaml` gets a `seed` service: same image, `command: ["/app/seed"]`, depending on a
     healthy postgres. The `api` service depends on `seed: service_completed_successfully`, so
     a failed seed fails `docker compose up` loudly. The database URL is defined once, in an
     `x-` extension field.
   - The Makefile gets `make seed`, which runs on the host and needs `.env` and `make db`.

#### Tests

- **Unit tests** (`make test`):
  - **Determinism:** the same seed and `now` give an identical dataset; a different seed gives
    a different one.
  - **Timelines:** signup ≤ activity ≤ now, basket lines come after the last order, and status
    changes stay within their bounds.
  - **Scenarios:** every threshold above.
- **Integration tests** (`make test-integration`, in throwaway databases):
  - **Customer and commerce stores:** rows round-trip intact: IDs, the optional phone,
    backdated timestamps and statuses.
  - **`seed.Run`:**
    - inserted counts match the generated dataset;
    - a second run skips;
    - two concurrent runs seed only once;
    - scenario 1 returns customers when run as SQL.

#### Docs

- **DECISIONS.md:** a new entry 18 (seeding). Entry 9's startup order is amended, and the open
  question about seed data is marked answered.
- **README.md:**
  - The quick start mentions the demo data and how to reseed (`docker compose down -v`).
  - `make seed` is documented.
  - The basket-history item in further improvements becomes an explicit limitation.
    `basket_items` holds only open lines, so "added X, then bought it" and "abandoned, then
    recovered" can't be segmented.
- **CLAUDE.md:** `make seed` in the Commands table.
- **PHASE_REVIEWS.md:** a Phase 2B entry.

#### Verification

1. **Checks:** `make check`, then `make db && make test-integration`.
2. **Fresh stack:** `docker compose down -v && docker compose up --build --wait`.
   - The seed logs its counts and duration.
   - The api and web services become healthy. This also confirms that `--wait` accepts a
     completed one-shot service.
3. **Idempotency:** a second `docker compose up` logs "already seeded", and the row counts stay
   the same.
4. **Query plan:** run EXPLAIN ANALYZE on the Phase 2A example segment against the seeded data.
   Phase 2A deferred this check until data existed.

↳ result

```
# Phase 2B: Customer and Commerce persistence, and synthetic data seeding

## Context

Phase 2A delivered the Customer and Commerce schema and domain model, but nothing writes to
them yet. Phase 2B adds two things:

- the insert-only GORM persistence that seeding needs;
- a deterministic, idempotent seeder.

The seeder fills an empty database with about 5,000 synthetic customers in TR, DE and GB, with
their permissions, baskets and orders. Segmentation and campaigns then have realistic data to
work on.

Out of scope: Campaign, Segmentation, Delivery and AI. Basket history is also out of scope,
and its limitation gets documented as a TODO.

## Decisions, and where I push back

| Topic | Decision | Alternative rejected |
|---|---|---|
| Where seeding runs | A new `cmd/seed` binary, run by a one-shot Compose service. Startup becomes postgres → seed → api → web, so the API starts with data | Seeding inside the API on startup puts demo-data code in the production binary and slows every start |
| Idempotency | One transaction that takes a PostgreSQL advisory lock, then skips if any customer exists. Reseed with `docker compose down -v` | Upserts on natural keys (orders have none); truncate-and-reseed (destroys data) |
| Determinism | A fixed random seed. Every timestamp is relative to the seeding time, which is passed in as `now`. The same seed and `now` always produce the identical dataset | Fixed absolute dates: the data would age, and "last 30 days" segments would empty out |
| Efficiency | GORM `CreateInBatches` in batches of 1,000 rows, inserted in dependency order with IDs returned. That is about 70k rows in a few seconds | COPY or parallel inserts: not needed at this size, and they bypass GORM |
| Realism | Hand-written lists of names, cities and products, plus six behaviour personas | A faker library: a new dependency with poor Turkish data, and random data doesn't guarantee segment coverage |
| Persistence | Insert-only `Store` types in `customer` and `commerce`, with unexported GORM row structs. The domain types stay unchanged, and the seeder owns the transaction | Full repositories with reads and updates: nothing calls them yet |
| Contact data | Emails use the reserved `example.com`, `example.net` and `example.org` domains (RFC 2606). Phone numbers are synthetic | Faker data that could match real people |

## Dataset (5,000 customers by default)

### Geography and language

| Country | Share | Cities | Languages |
|---|---|---|---|
| TR | 60% | 12 (İstanbul is about 35% of TR) | `tr` 92%, `en` 8% |
| DE | 25% | 8 | `de` 70%, `tr` 25% (the Turkish diaspora), `en` 5% |
| GB | 15% | 8 | `en` 90%, `tr` 10% |

Names follow the customer's language.

### Contact details and permissions

- About 90% of customers have a phone: TR 95%, DE 80%, GB 85%.
- For each channel a customer can be reached on:
  - 85% were asked for permission.
  - Of those asked, 70% granted email and 55% granted SMS.
  - 10% of grants were later revoked.
- A customer who was never asked has no row, which means "not permitted".

### Catalogue

8 categories × 3 brands × 7 items, about 170 products, priced in TRY (kuruş).

### Personas

| Persona | Share | Signed up | Completed orders | Last order | Open basket |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| New | 8% | last 30 days | 0–1 | — | 50% |

| Regular | 20% | 6+ months ago | 4–9 | last 60 days | 25% |
| Loyal | 10% | 1+ year ago | 10–15 | last 14 days | 30% |
| Lapsed | 15% | 1+ year ago | 2–6 | 6–18 months ago | 15% |

- **Order lines:**
  - 1 to 4 distinct products per order, 70% of them from the customer's 1–2 favourite
    categories.
  - Quantities are mostly 1.
  - The unit price is the product's price.
- **Order status:**
  - 90% completed;
  - 6% cancelled, 1–3 days after the order;
  - 4% refunded, 7–30 days after the order, never in the future.

  - 1 to 4 lines, all added after the customer's last order.

## Changes

1. **Shared helpers** (small refactors of existing code):
   - `internal/platform/config`: a `Config.NewLogger(w)` method, moved from `cmd/api/main.go`

5. **Compose and Makefile:**
   - `compose.yaml` gets a `seed` service: same image, `command: ["/app/seed"]`, depending on a
     healthy postgres. The `api` service depends on `seed: service_completed_successfully`, so

## Tests

- **Unit tests** (`make test`):

  - The quick start mentions the demo data and how to reseed (`docker compose down -v`).

  - The basket-history item in further improvements becomes an explicit limitation.
```

```
// Package config loads the configuration of the api and seed commands from environment
// variables.
//
// The process environment is the only source: the binaries never read .env files.
// Loading fails fast and reports every missing or invalid variable at once.
```

**Edit** `<repo>/internal/platform/config/config.go`

```
// NewLogger returns a logger that writes to w in the configured format, from the configured
// level up.
func (c Config) NewLogger(w io.Writer) *slog.Logger {
	opts := &slog.HandlerOptions{Level: c.LogLevel}
	if c.LogFormat == LogFormatText {
		return slog.New(slog.NewTextHandler(w, opts))
	}
	return slog.New(slog.NewJSONHandler(w, opts))
}

// LogValue implements slog.LogValuer, so the configuration can be logged without leaking
// the database credentials.
```

```
func newTestDatabase(t *testing.T) *sql.DB {
	t.Helper()

	}
```

```
## 9. Single-command startup with Docker Compose

**Context.** The assessment requires `docker compose up` to start the application, and the
final system will also include the frontend, seed data and mock providers.

**Decision.** `compose.yaml` runs `postgres`, `api` and `web`. Every variable has a development
default, so no `.env` is needed. Healthchecks order startup: postgres → api (`/readyz`) → web.
The backend image builds every `./cmd/*` binary, so future one-shot or long-running services
```

I've implemented and verified Phase 2B; nothing is committed yet. The full review is the new entry at the top of [PHASE_REVIEWS.md](PHASE_REVIEWS.md).

**What happens now:** on `docker compose up`, a one-shot `seed` service runs between postgres and the API. On a fresh volume it applies the migrations and seeds the data in 1.2 s; from an empty volume, the whole stack is healthy in about 15 s. The API only starts once seeding has succeeded. Later starts log "nothing to seed" and leave the data as it is.

**What was built**
- **Stores:** insert-only GORM stores in [customer/store.go](internal/customer/store.go) and [commerce/store.go](internal/commerce/store.go), with a shared 1,000-row batch insert. The domain types are unchanged.
- **Seeder:** [internal/seed](internal/seed/) and [cmd/seed](cmd/seed/main.go).
  - It builds everything through the domain constructors, so the Phase 2A rules hold.
  - Six customer personas make sure every useful segment exists in the data.
  - Emails use domains reserved for examples, and phone numbers are synthetic.
  - It works in one transaction behind a database lock, so it never seeds twice, even if two runs start at once.
  - The same seed always produces the same data, with timestamps relative to the seeding time.
- **Two small refactors:** a shared `NewLogger` for both commands, and a shared helper that gives integration tests a throwaway database.

**The dataset:** 5,000 customers, 8,059 permission rows, 168 products, 18,791 orders, 35,488 order lines and 3,560 basket lines. The tests guarantee the key segments. One example: 56 İstanbul customers who added to their basket in the last 30 days, have no completed order since, and allow SMS. The same query run as SQL on the seeded data returns those 56 in 1.5 ms, using the planned indexes.

**Verification**
- `make check` and `make test-integration` pass.
- The integration tests check that the database contains exactly what the generator produced, and that a second or concurrent run doesn't seed again.
- I recreated the Compose volume as part of the plan's verification. It held no customers beforehand, so nothing was lost.

**Worth a closer look**
- A failing seed now blocks `docker compose up`. That's on purpose, so the demo is never empty.
- Data in a kept volume ages: after a few weeks, "last 30 days" segments shrink. `docker compose down -v` refreshes them, and a reset option is on the README's improvements list.
- The basket-history limitation is now spelled out in the README. Only open basket lines are stored, so "added X, then bought it" can't be segmented.

**Proposed commits** (nothing was pre-staged this time)
1. `refactor: share logger setup and throwaway test databases`: config, `cmd/api`, `databasetest` and `migrations_test.go` (5 files)
2. `feat: add insert-only customer and commerce stores`: the `database.Insert` helper and both stores with their tests (5 files)
3. `feat(seed): generate deterministic synthetic customers and shopping activity`: `internal/seed` and `cmd/seed` (7 files)
4. `build: seed the database from a one-shot Compose service`: `compose.yaml` and `Makefile` (2 files)
5. `docs: record phase 2B seeding decision, review and limitations`: CLAUDE.md, DECISIONS.md, PHASE_REVIEWS.md and README.md (4 files)

Reply "commit" for these five, or "single commit" for one `feat: seed synthetic customers and activity (phase 2B)`.


**Plan presented for approval:**

### Coded errors: `internal/platform/apperr`

#### Context

Today the domain packages return `errors.New` sentinels wrapped in English text, such as
`invalid quantity 0: must be above zero`. Later, the API boundary must translate errors and
map them to HTTP statuses, and for that it needs stable, machine-readable codes. So domain
code should return a code plus parameters, not a message.

This change adds a small shared error type and moves `customer` and `commerce` onto it.
Translations, HTTP mapping and response formatting stay out of scope.

#### Design: `internal/platform/apperr` (one file, about 70 lines)

```go
type Code string // e.g. "customer.unreachable"; never changes once published

type Error struct { code Code; params map[string]any; cause error } // unexported, so sentinels can't be mutated

func New(code Code) *Error                     // declares a sentinel: var ErrX = apperr.New("context.x")
func (e *Error) With(key string, v any) *Error // returns a copy with one parameter added
func (e *Error) Wrap(cause error) *Error       // returns a copy with an underlying cause
func (e *Error) Code() Code
func (e *Error) Params() map[string]any        // returns a copy
func (e *Error) Error() string                 // "commerce.status_transition (from=cancelled, to=refunded): cause"
func (e *Error) Unwrap() error                 // returns the cause, so errors.Is and errors.As see through it
func (e *Error) Is(target error) bool          // true when the codes match, so errors.Is ignores params and cause
```

##### Codes

- A code has the form `context.condition`, in snake_case, and is declared next to the code
  that returns it.
- Each condition gets its own code, so each will map to exactly one message and one status
  later.

##### Parameters

- Parameters carry dynamic details, such as a channel, a quantity or order statuses.
- They never hold personal data, because they end up in logs and, later, in messages.

##### `Error()`

- The text is for logs and developers, never for end users.
- Its output is deterministic: parameters are sorted by name.

##### Copies

- `With` and `Wrap` never modify their receiver, so package-level sentinels stay immutable.
  This follows CLAUDE.md's "no package-level mutable state" rule.

##### Errors without a code

- Store and infrastructure failures and startup config errors stay plain `fmt.Errorf` chains.
- Wrapping a coded error with `fmt.Errorf("…: %w", err)` keeps its code reachable.
- The API boundary will treat any error without a code as internal.

##### Rejected alternatives

- **One generic `invalid` code with a `field` parameter.** Fewer codes, but logs would read
  `commerce.invalid (field=at)`, and translations would also need a table of field labels.
- **A kind or category for HTTP status.** Mapping errors to HTTP is the boundary's job; add a
  category when that mapping exists.
- **A third-party library** (pkg/errors, cockroachdb/errors). The standard library already
  handles wrapping, `errors.Is` and `errors.As`.

#### Domain migration

The four existing sentinels go away: `ErrInvalid` in both packages, `ErrUnreachable` and
`ErrStatusTransition`. They are replaced by one sentinel per condition. Each
`fmt.Errorf("%w …", ErrInvalid, …)` becomes the matching sentinel, with `.With(…)` where a
value matters. Parameters are in braces.

| Package | Codes |
|---|---|
| `customer` (11) | `first_name_required`, `last_name_required`, `email_required`, `email_invalid`, `phone_invalid`, `city_required`, `language_unsupported` {language}, `signup_time_required`, `channel_unsupported` {channel}, `permission_before_signup`, `unreachable` {channel} |
| `commerce` (12) | `category_required`, `product_name_required`, `price_invalid` {price}, `customer_required`, `product_required`, `quantity_invalid` {quantity}, `time_required`, `order_empty`, `unit_price_invalid` {unit_price}, `duplicate_product` {product_id}, `status_change_before_order`, `status_transition` {from, to} |

- Every code is prefixed with its package name, e.g. `customer.email_invalid`.
- The stores, `internal/seed` and `config` don't change. The seeder's
  `fmt.Errorf("customer %d: %w", …)` keeps the codes reachable.

#### Tests

- **`internal/platform/apperr/apperr_test.go`** (table-driven). It checks that:
  - `Error()` formats correctly with and without parameters and a cause;
  - `errors.Is` matches by code, through parameters and `fmt.Errorf` wrapping, but never
    across codes;
  - `errors.Is` reaches a cause such as `context.Canceled`;
  - `errors.As` extracts `*Error` from a wrapped chain;
  - `With` and `Wrap` leave the sentinel unchanged;
  - `Params` returns a copy.
- **Domain tests:**
  - `wantErr bool` becomes `wantErr error` holding the exact sentinel, which is stricter than
    today's "some invalid" check.
  - One `errors.As` assertion per package checks parameters: the unreachable channel, and the
    status transition's `from` and `to`.
  - The existing check that errors never repeat a rejected email or phone stays.

#### Docs

- **DECISIONS.md:** entry 19, coded errors.
- **CLAUDE.md:** a new principle. Domain and service errors are `apperr` sentinels with stable
  codes. Parameters carry details but never personal data, and nothing below the API boundary
  produces user-facing text.
- **README.md:** `apperr` in the layout. Further improvements gets translations and HTTP
  mapping at the API boundary.
- **PHASE_REVIEWS.md:** an entry for this change.

#### Verification

1. **Checks:** `make check` (unit tests, including `apperr` and both domain packages, plus
   lint) and `make test-integration` (stores and seeding behave as before).
2. **Leftovers:** a grep confirms no `ErrInvalid` wrapping or English error text is left in
   `internal/customer` or `internal/commerce` outside the stores.

↳ error

````
# Phase reviews

What each development phase built, newest first: its components, how they were tested and
verified, how the result differs from the plan, and what deserves a closer look. Entries are
snapshots taken when a phase is finished; later phases add entries rather than rewriting old
ones. The reasoning behind each choice is in [DECISIONS.md](DECISIONS.md), setup and commands
are in [README.md](README.md).

## Phase 2B: Persistence and synthetic data (2026-11-14)

**Scope.**

- Insert-only GORM persistence for the Customer and Commerce contexts.
- A deterministic, idempotent seeder that fills an empty database with 5,000 synthetic
  customers in TR, DE and GB, with their permissions, baskets and orders. Compose runs it as a
  one-shot service before the API.

Not built yet:

- reads and updates, which nothing calls yet;
- basket history, now documented as a limitation;
- Campaign, Segmentation, Delivery and AI.

**Result.**

- `make check` and `make test-integration` pass.
- Starting from an empty volume, `docker compose up --build --wait` seeds the data in 1.2 s,
  and the whole stack is healthy in about 15 s.
- A second start leaves the data alone.

### How the pieces fit

```text
postgres ──healthy──► seed ──exit 0──► api ──healthy──► web
                       │
                       ├─ migrate (idempotent, locked)
                       ├─ one transaction: advisory lock → customers exist? → stop
                       └─ generate and insert, in dependency order:
                          countries, cities → categories, products → customers, permissions
                          → orders, order lines → basket lines
```

### Components

| Component | Location | What it does | Tests |
|---|---|---|---|
| Customer store | `internal/customer/store.go` | Inserts countries, cities, customers and their permissions; checks whether customers exist | 2 integration tests |
| Commerce store | `internal/commerce/store.go` | Inserts categories, products, orders with their lines, and basket lines | 1 integration test |
| Batch insert | `internal/platform/database` (`Insert`) | GORM `CreateInBatches` in batches of 1,000, IDs returned | Through the stores |
| Seeder | `internal/seed` | Catalogue, people, personas, generator and `Run` | 15 cases, 88.2 % (74.0 % without a database) |
| Seed command | `cmd/seed` | Migrates, seeds with the default options, logs the counts | End to end in Compose |
| Test database | `internal/platform/database/databasetest` | A throwaway, migrated database per test | Used by four packages |
| Logger | `internal/platform/config` (`NewLogger`) | Shared by both commands | 2 cases |
| Compose and Makefile | `compose.yaml`, `Makefile` | `seed` service before `api`; `make seed` | End to end |

With PostgreSQL available, coverage is 94.6 % for `customer` and 95.0 % for `commerce`.

#### Persistence (`internal/customer/store.go`, `internal/commerce/store.go`)

- **Insert-only operations.** Customer: `CreateCountries`, `CreateCities`, `CreateCustomers`,
  `HasCustomers`. Commerce: `CreateCategories`, `CreateProducts`, `CreateOrders`,
  `CreateBaskets`. Nothing reads or updates yet.
- **Row structs.** Unexported row structs with explicit table names map the domain types.
  GORM's automatic timestamps are off, so generated times are stored as given, and a missing
  phone is stored as NULL.
- **IDs.** PostgreSQL returns them, and they are written back into the aggregates or reference
  structs. Saving a customer or order that already has an ID fails.
- **Transactions.** Stores take a `*gorm.DB` that may be a transaction; the seeder owns the
  transaction.

#### Seeder (`internal/seed`)

- **Geography and catalogue** (`catalog.go`):
  - 3 countries and 28 cities, with population-like weights;
  - 8 categories × 3 brands × 7 items = 168 products, priced in TRY and ending in ,90.
- **People** (`people.go`):
  - 20 first and 20 last names per language;
  - emails such as `[REDACTED EMAIL]` on domains reserved for examples;
  - synthetic mobile numbers, made unique by the customer's sequence number.
- **Personas** (`generate.go`): new 8 %, browser 17 %, occasional 30 %, regular 20 %, loyal
  10 %, lapsed 15 %. Each has its own signup window, number and timing of orders, and chance of
  an open basket.
- **Determinism.**
  - One PCG random source with seed 20261114.
  - Times are whole seconds, relative to the seeding time.
  - The generator never iterates over a map.
- **`Run`.**
  - One transaction: take the advisory lock, then skip if customers exist.
  - `build` hands each batch to a `sink` that inserts it, so later batches get the IDs they
    refer to.
  - Tests use an in-memory sink that numbers the rows instead, so they exercise the same
    generation code.

#### Compose (`compose.yaml`)

- `seed` runs `/app/seed` from the backend image once postgres is healthy.
- `api` waits for `seed` to complete successfully.
- The database URL is defined once, in `x-database-url`.

### The dataset

| Rows | Count |
|---|---|
| Customers | 5,000 (designed split: TR 60 %, DE 25 %, GB 15 %) |
| Permission rows | 8,059 |
| Products | 168 |
| Orders | 18,791 (6.1 % cancelled, 3.7 % refunded) |
| Order lines | 35,488 |
| Basket lines | 3,560 |

The tests guarantee these segments. The counts are from the default data:

| Segment | Customers | Guaranteed |
|---|---|---|
| İstanbul, basket added in the last 30 days, no completed order since, SMS allowed | 56 | ≥ 25 |
| Never completed an order | 1,110 | ≥ 15 % |
| Lapsed: last completed order 180+ days ago | 1,404 | ≥ 10 % |
| Loyal: 10+ completed orders | 376 | ≥ 5 % |
| Signed up in the last 30 days | 398 | ≥ 5 % |
| German customers who prefer Turkish | 321 | ≥ 15 % of DE |
| No phone | 498 | ≥ 5 % |
| Withdrew a permission | 504 | ≥ 2 % |

Every city has customers, and every category has buyers in the last 90 days.

### Verification

| # | Check | Result |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `make check` | Pass: Go tests with `-race`; lint with 0 issues; frontend tests (6) and build |
| 2 | `make test-integration` | Pass. The stores round-trip their rows. `Run` inserts exactly the generated dataset. The example segment as SQL matches the in-memory count (56). A second run skips, and of two concurrent runs, only one seeds |
| 3 | Fresh stack: `docker compose down -v && docker compose up --build --wait` | Pass. The seed applied migrations 1–3, seeded in 1.233 s and exited 0. api and web became healthy. `--wait` accepts the completed one-shot service. About 15 s in total |
| 4 | Second `docker compose up` | Pass: the seed logged "nothing to seed" and the row counts are unchanged |
| 5 | `make seed` on the host | Pass: it applies migrations and leaves the seeded database alone |
| 6 | EXPLAIN ANALYZE of the example segment | Pass: 56 customers in 1.5 ms. It uses `customers_city_id_idx`, the permissions primary key and `orders_customer_id_ordered_at_idx`; `basket_items` is small enough for a sequential scan |

### Deviations from the plan

- **Logging:** `Run` returns a `Result` and `cmd/seed` logs it; `Run` no longer takes a logger.
- **Batch insert:** both stores use a shared `database.Insert` in the platform package instead
  of each having its own copy.
- **Permission rows:** there are 8,059 instead of the estimated ~6,500, because declined
  answers are stored too, as rows with `granted = false`.
- **Further improvements:** the README list gained "refresh aged demo data without removing
  the volume".

### Worth a closer look

- **The API now depends on the seeder.** A failing seed blocks `docker compose up`. That is
  deliberate, so the demo is never empty, but it ties API availability to demo data in
  Compose. A production deployment wouldn't run the seed service.
- **Data ages in a persisted volume.** Everything is relative to the seeding time, so weeks
  later the "last 30 days" segments shrink. `docker compose down -v` refreshes the data.
- **Reproducibility across Go versions.** The dataset is reproducible with a given Go version.
  If a future release changes how `math/rand/v2` derives values such as `IntN` from the
  source, the exact rows could change. The tests compare two runs with each other rather than
  with fixed values.
- **Realistic-looking phone numbers.** They are synthetic but sit in plausible mobile ranges,
  so only mock providers may ever use them.
- **No read code yet.** The segmentation phase will read these tables directly (DECISIONS.md
  §13).

## Phase 2A: Customer and Commerce (2026-11-13)

**Scope.** The Customer and Commerce bounded contexts: their schema (two migrations) and domain
model, shaped for segmentation queries and for the seeder. Decisions taken while planning:

- customers in several countries (TR, DE, GB), speaking `tr`, `de` or `en`;
- an optional phone;
- orders that are `completed`, `cancelled` or `refunded`, refunds always in full;
- bigint identity keys;
- every price in TRY.

Not built yet: persistence code and synthetic data (Phase 2B), the segmentation engine,
campaigns, AI and delivery.

**Result.** `make check` and `make test-integration` pass, and the running API applied both
migrations on startup.

### How the pieces fit

```text
countries ─< cities ─< customers ─< notification_permissions
                         │  │
                         │  └─< orders ─< order_items ─┐
                         │                             ├─> products ─> categories
                         └─< basket_items ─────────────┘
```

`─<` means "has many" and `─>` means "refers to". The first row is the Customer context; the
rest is Commerce, which refers to customers by ID. Nothing writes these tables yet:

- the seeder (Phase 2B) will write them, through the domain types;
- the segmentation engine will read them directly, in single SQL statements (DECISIONS.md §13).

### Components

| Component | Location | What it does | Tests |
|---|---|---|---|
| Customer schema | `migrations/00002_customer.sql` | Countries, cities, customers and their marketing permissions | Schema tests |
| Commerce schema | `migrations/00003_commerce.sql` | Categories, products, basket lines, orders and order lines | Schema tests |
| Customer domain | `internal/customer` | Customer aggregate with its permissions; Email, Phone, Language, Channel; Country, City | 42 cases, 98.6 % |
| Commerce domain | `internal/commerce` | Product, Basket and Order; Money; Category | 34 cases, 100 % |
| Schema tests | `migrations/migrations_test.go` | Every constraint, the delete rules and the migration round trip, against real PostgreSQL | 32 cases, via `make test-integration` |
| Tooling and docs | `Makefile`, `DECISIONS.md`, `README.md`, `CLAUDE.md` | `make test-integration`; decisions 13–17; the further-improvements list | — |

#### Schema (`migrations/00002_customer.sql`, `migrations/00003_commerce.sql`)

- **Keys.** Every table has an identity key, except `countries`, which is keyed by its ISO code.
  Composite primary keys make two rules structural: one permission per channel, and one line
  per product in baskets and orders.
- **CHECK constraints.** They mirror the domain's closed sets and ranges:
  - E.164 phones;
  - languages, channels and order statuses;
  - positive quantities and prices;
  - no status change before its order.
- **Deletes.** They cascade only to what belongs to the parent: permissions, basket lines and
  order lines. Deleting a customer with orders, or a product in use, fails.
- **Indexes.** They serve the segment joins:
  - `customers (city_id)`;
  - `orders (customer_id, ordered_at)`;
  - `basket_items (product_id)` and `order_items (product_id)`;
  - `products (category_id)`;
  - the unique `cities (country_code, name)`, which doubles as the country filter.

#### Customer domain (`internal/customer`)

- **New customers.** `New(profile, createdAt)` checks the trimmed names, the email, the city
  and the language. A new customer has no permissions, so every channel starts as not permitted.
- **Permissions.** `Grant`, `Revoke`, `Permitted` and `Permissions` manage marketing
  permissions.
  - Granting SMS to a customer without a phone fails with `ErrUnreachable`.
  - No change can predate the signup.
- **Contact details.**
  - `ParseEmail` trims, lowercases and accepts only a single plain address.
  - `ParsePhone` strips formatting and requires E.164.
  - Their errors never repeat the input, and they log masked values (`***@example.com`,
    `+90********67`).
- **Closed sets and reference data.** `Language` and `Channel` match the CHECK constraints;
  `Country` and `City` are plain reference data.

#### Commerce domain (`internal/commerce`)

- **Products.** `NewProduct` requires a category, a name and a price above zero. `Category` is
  plain reference data.
- **Baskets.** `Basket.Add` keeps one line per product: adding again raises the quantity and
  keeps the latest add time.
- **Orders.**
  - `NewOrder` requires lines: one per product, each with a quantity above zero and a unit
    price of zero or more. A new order starts `completed`.
  - `Cancel` and `Refund` work only from `completed`, only once, and never before the order
    time.
  - `Total` is computed. Lines are copied in and out, so callers can't change them.
- **Money.** An int64 amount in kuruş.

#### Schema tests (`migrations/migrations_test.go`)

- **Skipped by default.** They run only when `TEST_DATABASE_URL` is set, so `make test` and
  `make check` still need no database.
- **Throwaway databases.** Each test creates a `campaign_test_*` database, migrates it with the
  application's own `database.Open` and `database.Migrate`, and drops it afterwards.
- **Coverage.**
  - 30 table-driven cases each break one constraint and assert its name.
  - One test checks the cascading deletes.
  - One test migrates down to version 0 and back up.

### Verification

| # | Check | Result |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `make check` | Pass: Go tests with `-race`, `make lint` with 0 issues, frontend lint, type-check, 6 tests and build |
| 2 | `make test-integration` | Pass: all 30 constraint cases, the cascading deletes, and migrations down to 0 and back up. No test database was left behind |
| 3 | Migrations on startup | Pass: the rebuilt API logged versions 2 and 3 as applied, `make migrate-status` lists all three, and `/readyz` answers 200 through nginx |
| 4 | Example segment query | Pass: it runs on the real schema. On empty tables, EXPLAIN uses `customers_city_id_idx`, `basket_items_pkey`, `orders_customer_id_ordered_at_idx` and `notification_permissions_pkey` |
| 5 | Domain invariants | Pass: every invariant and status transition has a test case. The only uncovered statement is a defensive branch for unknown channels |

Not checked: query plans at realistic volumes, which need the seeder's data.

### Deviations from the plan

- **Additions:**
  - a CHECK that country codes are two capital letters;
  - the cascading-delete test;
  - a schema test case that a customer with orders can't be deleted.
- **Unplanned file:** `internal/commerce/commerce.go` holds the package documentation and errors.
- **No tests for reference data:** `Country`, `City` and `Category` are plain structs with no
  behaviour yet.
- **Personal data:** validation errors never repeat the rejected value, because it may be
  personal data.
- **CLAUDE.md:** two new conventions:
  - the no-personal-data rule now covers error messages;
  - domain code takes the current time as a parameter.

### Worth a closer look

- **Domain-only rules.** The database can't enforce these:
  - SMS permission needs a phone;
  - an order has lines;
  - the order status lifecycle.

  A writer that bypasses the domain types, such as raw SQL or psql, can break them, so the
  seeder must use the domain types.
- **Segmentation reads these tables directly** (DECISIONS.md §13). Renaming a column will break
  segment queries, so once those queries exist, their tests should run against this schema.
- **`make test-integration` isn't part of `make check`.** CI would need a PostgreSQL service.
  Until then, run it whenever migrations change.
- **Everything is priced in TRY,** including for customers in Germany and the UK. Spend segments
  are therefore only meaningful in TRY.
- **No persistence code yet.** The GORM mapping arrives with the seeder, and IDs stay 0 until
  then.

What this phase deferred is on README.md's further-improvements list. What "haven't purchased"
means in a segment is an open question in DECISIONS.md.

## Phase 1: Project foundation (2026-11-13)

**Scope.** A runnable skeleton without business features. The Go API connects to PostgreSQL,
applies migrations, serves health probes, logs structured JSON and shuts down gracefully. The
Vue app shows whether the API is ready. `docker compose up --build` starts everything on a fresh
clone without a `.env`. Not built yet, by design: domain entities, campaign logic, AI
integration, queues and workers, notification channels, seed data and mock providers.

**Result.** All 12 acceptance criteria pass, and `make check` is green.

### How the pieces fit

```text
browser
  │
  ▼
web        nginx on host port 3000: serves the built Vue app, proxies /api/* and /readyz
  │
  ▼
api        Go on host port 8080: applies migrations on startup, serves the health probes
  │
  ▼
postgres   PostgreSQL 18 on host port 5432, data in the pgdata volume
```

Compose starts them bottom-up, each once the one below is healthy. In development, Vite on
:5173 replaces nginx and the API runs on the host (`make run`). To read the Go code, start at
`cmd/api/main.go`: it calls every other component in start-up order.

### Components

| Component | Location | What it does | Tests |
|---|---|---|---|
| API entry point | `cmd/api` | Wires everything together; refuses to start on bad config or an unreachable database | Manual (verification 5–6) |
| Configuration | `internal/platform/config` | Typed settings from environment variables | 9 cases, 100 % |
| HTTP server | `internal/platform/httpserver` | Router, request IDs, access log, panic recovery, timeouts, graceful shutdown | 10 cases, 93 % |
| Health probes | `internal/platform/health` | `/healthz` (liveness) and `/readyz` (readiness) | 5 cases, 100 % |
| Database and migrations | `internal/platform/database`, `migrations/` | Connection pool, start-up ping, versioned schema migrations | Manual (verification 3–4, 6) |
| Web app | `frontend/src` | Vue shell with an API status badge | 6 Vitest tests |
| Web server | `frontend/Dockerfile`, `frontend/nginx.conf` | Serves the built app and proxies API calls | Manual (verification 1) |
| Containers | `Dockerfile`, `compose.yaml`, `.dockerignore` | One-command stack with health-ordered start-up | Manual (verification 1, 3, 12) |
| Developer tooling | `Makefile`, `.golangci.yml`, `.env.example`, `.nvmrc`, `.gitignore` | Development loop, linting, migrations, `make check` | Manual (verification 8–11) |
| Documentation | `README.md`, `DECISIONS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, this file | Setup, reasoning, working rules, phase reviews | — |

Go test cases count each table-test row once; percentages are statement coverage from
`go test -cover`.

#### API entry point (`cmd/api/main.go`)

- `main` turns SIGINT and SIGTERM into context cancellation and exits 1 on error. `run` does all
  the wiring, so every dependency is explicit and there is no global state.
- Start-up order: configuration → logger → database (5 s ping) → migrations → probes → listener →
  server. Any failure stops the process before it accepts traffic.
- The configuration is logged at start-up without the database URL.

#### Configuration (`internal/platform/config`)

- `Load` parses `DATABASE_URL` (required), `HTTP_ADDR` (default `:8080`), `LOG_LEVEL` (default
  `info`) and `LOG_FORMAT` (`json` or `text`, default `json`) with caarlos0/env. It reports every
  missing or invalid variable in one error.
- It reads only the variables passed in (`main` passes the process environment) and never a
  `.env` file, so tests never depend on the process environment.
- `Config` implements `slog.LogValuer`, which leaves the database URL out of logs.

#### HTTP server (`internal/platform/httpserver`)

- `NewRouter` returns a chi router with three middlewares, outermost first: request ID (reusing
  an incoming `X-Request-Id`), access log and panic recovery.
- The access log writes one line per request with `request_id`, `method`, `path`, `status`,
  `bytes` and `duration_ms`: 5xx at error level, successful health probes at debug level.
- Panic recovery logs the panic and its stack as structured JSON and answers 500. It runs inside
  the access log, so the failed request is still logged. `http.ErrAbortHandler` is re-panicked,
  as net/http expects.
- `Serve` applies server timeouts (header 5 s, read 15 s, write 30 s, idle 60 s). When its
  context is cancelled, it stops accepting connections and gives in-flight requests up to 10 s.
  Request contexts don't derive from the signal, so a shutdown never cancels them.

#### Health probes (`internal/platform/health`)

- `GET /healthz` always answers 200 and checks nothing, so a database outage never gets a
  healthy process restarted.
- `GET /readyz` runs every registered check (today, a PostgreSQL ping) under a 2 s timeout. It
  answers 200, or 503 with the state of each check, e.g.
  `{"status":"unavailable","checks":{"postgres":"unavailable"}}`. Error details are logged,
  never returned.
- A check is a plain `func(ctx) error`, so a new dependency is one more entry in `cmd/api`.

#### Database and migrations (`internal/platform/database`, `migrations/`)

- `Open` connects through GORM (pgx driver) with explicit pool limits (20 open and idle
  connections, 30 min lifetime, 5 min idle time), pings within 5 s and closes the pool if the
  ping fails.
- GORM logs through slog at warn level and above: errors and queries slower than 200 ms, as
  parameterised SQL, so bind values (personal data) never reach the logs. Database errors are
  translated, e.g. a unique violation becomes `gorm.ErrDuplicatedKey`.
- `Migrate` applies the embedded goose SQL migrations under a PostgreSQL advisory lock, so several
  instances can start at once, and logs each applied file and the resulting version.
- `00001_baseline.sql` is a deliberate no-op that starts version tracking; the first real tables
  arrive with the first business capability.

#### Web app (`frontend/src`)

- Vue 3.5, TypeScript 6 and Vite 8, scaffolded by create-vue with Vitest, ESLint, Oxlint and
  Prettier. No router, store or component library yet.
- `ApiStatus.vue` calls `/readyz` once when it mounts and shows "API: ready", "API: not ready"
  (503) or "API: unreachable" (any other status or a network error, including the development
  proxy's 502 when the API is down).
- `vite.config.ts` proxies `/api` and `/readyz` to `localhost:8080`, so the browser stays on one
  origin and the API needs no CORS.

#### Web server (`frontend/Dockerfile`, `frontend/nginx.conf`)

- Node 26 builds the app; `nginx-unprivileged` 1.30 serves it as a non-root user.
- Unknown paths fall back to `index.html` for client-side routing. `/api/` and `/readyz` are
  proxied to the API with `X-Request-Id`, `X-Forwarded-For` and `X-Forwarded-Proto`.
- nginx looks `api` up in Docker's DNS on each request, so it still reaches a recreated API
  container with a new IP (verified).

#### Containers (`Dockerfile`, `compose.yaml`, `.dockerignore`)

- The backend image builds every `./cmd/*` binary and runs on `alpine:3.24` as uid 10001. Later
  programs (seed, worker, mock provider) become new Compose services reusing this image.
- Compose starts postgres, then api, then web, each once the previous one is healthy
  (`pg_isready`, then `/readyz`). Every setting has a development default, so a `.env` is
  optional and only overrides ports and credentials.
- `stop_grace_period: 15s` leaves room for the API's 10 s shutdown. PostgreSQL 18 keeps its data
  under `/var/lib/postgresql/18/docker`, so the volume is mounted at `/var/lib/postgresql`.
- The root `.dockerignore` keeps `.env`, `.git` and the frontend out of the API build;
  `frontend/.dockerignore` keeps the host's `node_modules` and `dist` out of the web build.

#### Developer tooling (`Makefile`, `.golangci.yml`, `.env.example`, `.nvmrc`, `.gitignore`)

- `make` lists every target: the stack (`up`, `down`, `logs`, `db`, `run`), Go (`test`, `lint`,
  `fmt`), migrations (`migrate-*`), frontend (`web-*`) and `check`, which runs all checks.
- Host runs load `.env` and export only its keys. Targets stop with a hint when `.env` is missing
  or Node is too old. Migration commands aren't echoed, so the database password stays off the
  terminal.
- `.env.example` documents every variable with development values; `.nvmrc` pins Node 26.
- golangci-lint v2.15.0 is pinned and built into `./bin`. It runs the standard linters plus
  `bodyclose`, `errorlint`, `gosec`, `noctx` and `revive`, and formats with `gofmt` and
  `goimports`.
- `.gitignore` now also applies its editor and test-output rules inside `frontend/`, and ignores
  Vite's temporary files.

### Verification

| # | Criterion | Result |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Fresh clone without `.env`: `docker compose up --build` | Pass: postgres and api healthy, web running; the page, `/readyz` and the SPA fallback answer through nginx |
| 2 | Liveness | Pass: `/healthz` answers 200 `{"status":"ok"}` |
| 3 | Readiness follows the database | Pass: with PostgreSQL stopped, `/readyz` 503 and `/healthz` 200, api unhealthy after ~22 s; 200 again ~2 s after the restart |
| 4 | Migrations | Pass: v1 applied once and skipped on restart; `migrate-status`, `migrate-down`, `migrate-up` and `migrate-new` work |
| 5 | Graceful shutdown | Pass: `docker compose stop api` exits 0; the in-flight request test passes |
| 6 | Fail-fast configuration | Pass: missing `DATABASE_URL` exits 1 and all errors are reported together; an unreachable database fails within 5 s |
| 7 | Structured logs | Pass: JSON lines with the access-log fields; request IDs match nginx's |
| 8 | Go quality | Pass: `make test` with `-race`, `make lint` with 0 issues; no `frontend/` code in `go list ./...` |
| 9 | Development loop | Pass: `make db`, `make run`, `make web-dev`; with the API stopped, the proxy's 502 maps to "unreachable" |
| 10 | Frontend quality | Pass: lint, type-check, 6 tests and production build |
| 11 | Nothing secret or generated is tracked | Pass: `.env`, `bin/`, `node_modules/`, `dist/` and local editor settings ignored; `.env.example` tracked |
| 12 | Non-root containers | Pass: api runs as uid 10001, web as uid 101 |

Not checked: the page in a real browser. Its rendering is covered by the Vitest tests.

### Deviations from the plan

- Go 1.27.4 comes from the `toolchain` line in `go.mod`, not a system-wide install.
- golangci-lint is pinned and built into `./bin` rather than installed globally.
- `httpserver.Serve` takes a listener (the plan had `Run(ctx)`), so tests can use a random port
  and the log shows the real address.
- The access-log field is `duration_ms`, not `duration`.
- `.gitignore` changed, although the plan expected no change: nested-directory rules were fixed
  and Vite's temporary files added.
- Not in the plan: migration targets don't echo their commands, and nginx sets `X-Request-Id`
  so web and API logs share request IDs.
- create-vue now also sets up Oxlint.

### Worth a closer look

- **GORM** was chosen for speed, with guardrails ([DECISIONS.md](DECISIONS.md) §5). Query
  mistakes surface at runtime rather than at compile time, so every repository will need
  integration tests.
- **No automated tests for `database` and `cmd/api`** (0 % coverage). Both were verified by
  running the stack; integration tests against a real PostgreSQL should arrive with the first
  repository.
- **Migrations run at API start-up**, so the API's database role needs DDL rights (§6). That is
  fine here; in production, migrations would run as a separate job.
- **Server-sent events**, if chosen for live delivery status, need the 30 s write deadline
  extended per stream (`http.ResponseController`), streams ended on shutdown, and
  `proxy_buffering off` in nginx.

Open questions for later phases, including the queue choice, are recorded in
[DECISIONS.md](DECISIONS.md).
````

### Phase 3: Campaign management

#### Context

Customers and their shopping data exist (Phases 2A and 2B); campaigns don't. Phase 3 adds the
campaign domain:

- a lifecycle that starts at DRAFT;
- a segment rule DSL that the next phase's engine will compile into SQL;
- channel-specific content with validation;
- a REST API that manages drafts only.

The same application service will serve both manual editing and, later, AI proposals.

Out of scope:

- sending, scheduling, queues and segment queries;
- AI and the frontend;
- the shared error framework.

#### Important design decisions

##### 1. Lifecycle: five statuses, transitions defined now, only drafts change

| From | To | Future trigger |
|---|---|---|
| draft | scheduled | approve with a send time |
| draft | sending | approve and send now |
| scheduled | draft | unschedule, so it can be edited again |
| scheduled | sending | the send time arrives |
| scheduled | cancelled | cancel before sending |
| sending | sent | every message has been handed to a provider |

- `sent` and `cancelled` are final.
- The transition table lives in the domain and has tests.
- The database CHECK lists all five statuses, so later phases only add timestamp columns.
- Only drafts can be updated or deleted. Deletion is a hard delete.
- No send or schedule endpoints exist until those capabilities do.

##### 2. One application service for manual and AI campaigns

- `campaign.Service` exposes Create, Update, Get, List and Delete. Each takes a `Draft`: name,
  segment and content.
- The HTTP handlers are thin adapters around the service.
- The AI phase will build the same `Draft` from model output, so both paths share one set of
  rules.
- The service depends on a small `repository` interface. The GORM store implements it, and the
  API tests use an in-memory fake (a test seam, as CLAUDE.md allows).

##### 3. Content: one optional block per channel

```json
"content": {
  "sms":   {"body": "Merhaba {{first_name}}, sepetin seni bekliyor!"},
  "email": {"subject": "{{first_name}}, sepetini unutma", "body": "…"}
}
```

- The campaign's channels follow from which blocks are present. This uses `customer.Channel`,
  so a campaign can't name a channel without content for it.
- The database stores the content in nullable columns: `sms_body`, `email_subject` and
  `email_body`.
- Multilingual campaigns, a later phase, will move content into a table keyed by channel and
  language.

##### 4. Validation: every problem reported at once

| Field | Rule |
|---|---|
| `name` | Required, at most 100 characters |
| `content` | At least one channel |
| `content.sms.body` | Required, at most 160 characters |
| `content.email.subject` | Required, at most 80 characters |
| `content.email.body` | Required |
| Any text | Only `{{first_name}}`, `{{last_name}}` and `{{city}}` are allowed; every `{{` must be closed |
| `segment` | Valid DSL, version 1 |

- **Characters** are Unicode characters, not bytes.
- **Templates are measured as written,** with a placeholder counting as its own text. Rendered
  length depends on the customer, so the delivery phase checks it.
- **A caveat on the 160-character limit.** It is the GSM-7 single-message limit. Turkish
  letters such as ş, ğ and ı switch a message to UCS-2, where one segment holds only 70
  characters. I'll enforce 160 as asked and list segment-aware limits as a further improvement.
- **All problems at once.** Validation returns every problem together, each with a field path
  and a stable code, for example
  `{"field":"content.sms.body","problem":"too_long"}`. Forms can show every error, and the AI
  phase can send the whole list back to the model for repair.

##### 5. Segment DSL v1: typed conditions, whitelisted by construction

```json
{"version": 1, "match": "all", "conditions": [
  {"type": "city", "in": ["İstanbul"]},
  {"type": "basket", "within_days": 30},
  {"type": "purchased", "within_days": 30, "negate": true}
]}
```

| `type` | Parameters | Matches customers who… |
|---|---|---|
| `country` / `city` / `language` | `in` | live in, or prefer, one of the listed values (ISO codes, city names, `tr`/`de`/`en`) |
| `signed_up` | `within_days` | signed up in the last N days |
| `basket` | `within_days`?, `categories`? | have open basket lines, optionally recent and/or from given categories |
| `purchased` | `within_days`?, `categories`? | have a completed order, optionally recent and/or in given categories |
| `order_count` | `within_days`?, `min`?, `max`? | have a number of completed orders within the range |
| `total_spent` | `within_days`?, `min_try`?, `max_try`? | spent, in whole TRY, an amount within the range |
| `group` | `match`, `conditions` | nest `all`/`any` logic |

- **One SQL fragment per condition.** Each type is one of the patterns from Phase 2A. The
  engine will compile it to a single parameterised fragment, typically an EXISTS subquery.
  Unknown types and parameters are rejected, as CLAUDE.md requires for untrusted input.
- **Negation.** Any condition accepts `negate: true`. "Haven't purchased in 30 days" is
  `purchased` with `within_days: 30` and `negate: true`. That settles the open question in
  DECISIONS.md: "not since the basket add" isn't expressible in version 1.
- **Limits.** Depth is at most 3, with at most 20 conditions, lists of at most 50 values, and
  windows of 1–3,650 days. An empty root (`"conditions": []`) targets every customer.
- **Consent is implicit.** It is never a condition: the engine will always require permission
  for each channel the campaign uses.
- **Names, not IDs.** Cities and categories are referenced by name, which stays readable and
  stable across reseeds. Phase 3 checks only their format; the segmentation phase checks that
  they exist.

##### 6. Persistence

- **One `campaigns` table.** Columns: `id` (identity), `name`, `status`, `segment` (jsonb),
  the three nullable content columns, `created_at` and `updated_at`.
- **CHECK constraints.** The status set, at least one channel, an email subject only together
  with a body, the length limits, and `jsonb_typeof(segment) = 'object'`.
- **Conditional writes.** Updates and deletes run `… WHERE id = ? AND status = 'draft'`, so a
  status change in a later phase can't race an edit. Partial updates use `Select`, as CLAUDE.md
  requires.

##### 7. API conventions

| Request | Responses |
|---|---|
| `POST /api/campaigns` | 201 with the campaign and a `Location` header; 422 if invalid |
| `GET /api/campaigns` | 200 with every campaign, newest first |
| `GET /api/campaigns/{id}` | 200, or 404 |
| `PUT /api/campaigns/{id}` | 200 after replacing name, segment and content; 404; 409 if not a draft; 422 |
| `DELETE /api/campaigns/{id}` | 204; 404; 409 if not a draft |

- **Bodies.** Responses include `id`, `status`, `channels`, `segment`, `content`,
  `created_at` and `updated_at`.
- **Request limits.** Bodies over 64 KB get 413. Unknown fields or malformed JSON get 400.
- **Error bodies.** They live in the campaign handlers, e.g. `{"error":"not_draft"}` or
  `{"error":"invalid_campaign","problems":[…]}`. Moving them to the API boundary is a job for
  the error framework later.
- **No pagination yet.**
- **Concurrent edits.** For drafts, the last write wins. Optimistic locking arrives with
  approval, when concurrent transitions start to matter.

#### Decisions for you (recommendation first)

1. **When validation applies.**
   - (a) Every save must be complete and valid. One rule, AI proposals are complete anyway, and
     every draft is ready to approve.
   - (b) Incomplete drafts may be saved, with full validation only on approval. This needs two
     levels of rules.
2. **How SMS length is counted.**
   - (a) The template as written.
   - (b) Each placeholder reserves a fixed width, e.g. 20 characters.
   - (c) GSM-7/UCS-2 segment counting.
3. **Placeholders.**
   - (a) `first_name`, `last_name` and `city`.
   - (b) Only `first_name`.
4. **Checking that city and category names exist.**
   - (a) In the segmentation phase, which keeps campaigns free of customer and commerce reads
     for now.
   - (b) Now, by adding read methods to both stores.

If you approve without comments, I'll use the recommended options.

#### Implementation

```text
internal/campaign/
  campaign.go   Campaign aggregate, Status and its transitions, Draft, errors
  content.go    Content, SMS, Email, placeholder parsing, length limits
  segment.go    Segment DSL v1 types and validation
  problems.go   Problem {field, code}, the joined validation error
  service.go    Service and its repository interface
  store.go      GORM store (jsonb segment, conditional update and delete)
  http.go       handlers, JSON shapes, error responses
migrations/00004_campaigns.sql            created with make migrate-new
internal/platform/httpserver/json.go      DecodeJSON (64 KB, strict) and WriteJSON; health switches to WriteJSON
internal/customer                         export Channel.Valid() and Language.Valid() for reuse
cmd/api/main.go                           mount /api/campaigns with Service(NewStore(db), time.Now)
```

#### Tests

- **Domain** (table-driven):
  - every transition pair, valid and invalid;
  - the draft-only rules;
  - every validation rule at its boundary, e.g. 160 vs 161 characters with Turkish letters;
  - placeholders: known, unknown and malformed;
  - every condition type, both valid and invalid, plus the DSL limits.
- **API** (`httptest`, real Service, fake repository):
  - every endpoint and status code;
  - the 422 problem list;
  - 400 for unknown fields and 413 for oversized bodies;
  - JSON shapes;
  - no route for send or schedule.
- **Persistence** (`make test-integration`):
  - a jsonb round trip, nullable content and timestamps;
  - updates and deletes that touch only drafts, shown by changing a status in SQL first;
  - newest-first ordering;
  - the new CHECK constraints in `migrations_test.go`.

#### Docs

- **DECISIONS.md:**
  - three entries: the lifecycle and draft-only API, the segment DSL v1, and content with its
    validation;
  - the "haven't purchased" question marked answered.
- **README.md:**
  - an API section with an example, plus status and layout updates;
  - further improvements: pagination, optimistic locking, segment-aware SMS limits, and the
    shared error framework.
- **PHASE_REVIEWS.md:** a Phase 3 entry.

#### Verification

2. **Running stack:** `docker compose up --build`, then curl through nginx:
   - create, list, get, update and delete a campaign;
   - a 422 with several problems;

   - confirm the seeded data is untouched.

````
## Context

Customers and their shopping data exist (Phases 2A and 2B); campaigns don't. Phase 3 adds the
campaign domain:

- a minimal lifecycle that starts at DRAFT;
- a segment rule DSL that the next phase's engine will compile into SQL;

Out of scope:

- sending, scheduling, queues and segment queries;
- AI and the frontend;
- the shared error framework.

## Important design decisions

### 1. Lifecycle: kept minimal, with three statuses

| From | To | Future trigger |

| draft | scheduled | approve with a send time |
| draft | sending | approve and send now |
| scheduled | draft | unschedule, so it can be edited again |
| scheduled | sending | the send time arrives |

  on) is decided when delivery is built; the delivery phase extends this table and the CHECK.
- **The rules live in the domain.** The transition table has tests, and the database CHECK

- The service depends on a small `repository` interface. The GORM store implements it, and the

### 3. Content: one optional block per channel

}

- The campaign's channels follow from which blocks are present. This uses `customer.Channel`,
  so a campaign can't name a channel without content for it.
- The database stores the content in nullable columns: `sms_body`, `email_subject` and

### 4. Validation: every problem reported at once

| Field | Rule |

| `content.email.body` | Required |
| Any text | Only `{{first_name}}`, `{{last_name}}` and `{{city}}` are allowed; every `{{` must be closed |
| `segment` | Valid DSL, version 1 |

- **Characters** are Unicode characters, not bytes.
- **Templates are measured as written,** with a placeholder counting as its own text. Rendered
  length depends on the customer, so the delivery phase checks it.
- **A caveat on the 160-character limit.** It is the GSM-7 single-message limit. Turkish
  letters such as ş, ğ and ı switch a message to UCS-2, where one segment holds only 70
  characters. I'll enforce 160 as asked and list segment-aware limits as a further improvement.
- **All problems at once.** Validation returns every problem together, each with a field path
  and a stable code, for example
  `{"field":"content.sms.body","problem":"too_long"}`. Forms can show every error, and the AI
  phase can send the whole list back to the model for repair.

### 5. Segment DSL v1: typed conditions, whitelisted by construction

```json
{"version": 1, "match": "all", "conditions": [
  {"type": "city", "in": ["İstanbul"]},
  {"type": "basket", "within_days": 30},
  {"type": "purchased", "within_days": 30, "negate": true}
]}
```

| `type` | Parameters | Matches customers who… |
|---|---|---|
| `country` / `city` / `language` | `in` | live in, or prefer, one of the listed values (ISO codes, city names, `tr`/`de`/`en`) |
| `signed_up` | `within_days` | signed up in the last N days |
| `basket` | `within_days`?, `categories`? | have open basket lines, optionally recent and/or from given categories |
| `purchased` | `within_days`?, `categories`? | have a completed order, optionally recent and/or in given categories |
| `order_count` | `within_days`?, `min`?, `max`? | have a number of completed orders within the range |
| `total_spent` | `within_days`?, `min_try`?, `max_try`? | spent, in whole TRY, an amount within the range |
| `group` | `match`, `conditions` | nest `all`/`any` logic |

- **One SQL fragment per condition.** Each type is one of the patterns from Phase 2A. The
  engine will compile it to a single parameterised fragment, typically an EXISTS subquery.
  Unknown types and parameters are rejected, as CLAUDE.md requires for untrusted input.
- **Negation.** Any condition accepts `negate: true`. "Haven't purchased in 30 days" is
  `purchased` with `within_days: 30` and `negate: true`. That settles the open question in
  DECISIONS.md: "not since the basket add" isn't expressible in version 1.
- **Limits.** Depth is at most 3, with at most 20 conditions, lists of at most 50 values, and
  windows of 1–3,650 days. An empty root (`"conditions": []`) targets every customer.
- **Consent is implicit.** It is never a condition: the engine will always require permission
  for each channel the campaign uses.
- **Names, not IDs.** Cities and categories are referenced by name, which stays readable and
  stable across reseeds. Phase 3 checks only their format; the segmentation phase checks that
  they exist.

### 6. Persistence

- **One `campaigns` table.** Columns: `id` (identity), `name`, `status`, `segment` (jsonb),
  the three nullable content columns, `created_at` and `updated_at`.
- **CHECK constraints.** The status set (`draft`, `scheduled`, `sending`), at least one
  channel, an email subject only together with a body, the length limits, and
  `jsonb_typeof(segment) = 'object'`.
- **Conditional writes.** Updates and deletes run `… WHERE id = ? AND status = 'draft'`, so a
  status change in a later phase can't race an edit. Partial updates use `Select`, as CLAUDE.md
  requires.

### 7. API conventions

| Request | Responses |
|---|---|
| `POST /api/campaigns` | 201 with the campaign and a `Location` header; 422 if invalid |
| `GET /api/campaigns` | 200 with every campaign, newest first |
| `GET /api/campaigns/{id}` | 200, or 404 |
| `PUT /api/campaigns/{id}` | 200 after replacing name, segment and content; 404; 409 if not a draft; 422 |
| `DELETE /api/campaigns/{id}` | 204; 404; 409 if not a draft |

- **Bodies.** Responses include `id`, `status`, `channels`, `segment`, `content`,
  `created_at` and `updated_at`.
- **Request limits.** Bodies over 64 KB get 413. Unknown fields or malformed JSON get 400.
- **Error bodies.** They live in the campaign handlers, e.g. `{"error":"not_draft"}` or
  `{"error":"invalid_campaign","problems":[…]}`. Moving them to the API boundary is a job for
  the error framework later.
- **No pagination yet.**
- **Concurrent edits.** For drafts, the last write wins. Optimistic locking arrives with
  approval, when concurrent transitions start to matter.

## Decisions taken

1. **Every save must be complete and valid.** There is one level of validation, so every draft
   is ready to approve.
2. **SMS length counts the template as written.** Segment-aware counting goes on the
   further-improvements list.
3. **Placeholders:** `{{first_name}}`, `{{last_name}}` and `{{city}}`.
4. **City and category names are checked against reference data in the segmentation phase.**
   Phase 3 checks their format only.
5. **The lifecycle stays minimal:** `draft`, `scheduled` and `sending`. Delivery decides how a
   campaign finishes.

## Implementation

```text
internal/campaign/
  campaign.go   Campaign aggregate, Status and its transitions, Draft, errors
  content.go    Content, SMS, Email, placeholder parsing, length limits
  segment.go    Segment DSL v1 types and validation
  problems.go   Problem {field, code}, the joined validation error
  service.go    Service and its repository interface
  store.go      GORM store (jsonb segment, conditional update and delete)
  http.go       handlers, JSON shapes, error responses
migrations/00004_campaigns.sql            created with make migrate-new
internal/platform/httpserver/json.go      DecodeJSON (64 KB, strict) and WriteJSON; health switches to WriteJSON
internal/customer                         export Channel.Valid() and Language.Valid() for reuse
cmd/api/main.go                           mount /api/campaigns with Service(NewStore(db), time.Now)
```

## Tests

- **Domain** (table-driven):
  - all 9 transition pairs between the three statuses, valid and invalid;
  - the draft-only rules;
  - every validation rule at its boundary, e.g. 160 vs 161 characters with Turkish letters;
  - placeholders: known, unknown and malformed;
  - every condition type, both valid and invalid, plus the DSL limits.
- **API** (`httptest`, real Service, fake repository):
  - every endpoint and status code;
  - the 422 problem list;
  - 400 for unknown fields and 413 for oversized bodies;
  - JSON shapes;
  - no route for send or schedule.
- **Persistence** (`make test-integration`):
  - a jsonb round trip, nullable content and timestamps;
  - updates and deletes that touch only drafts, shown by changing a status in SQL first;
  - newest-first ordering;
  - the new CHECK constraints in `migrations_test.go`.

## Docs

- **DECISIONS.md:**
  - three entries: the lifecycle and draft-only API, the segment DSL v1, and content with its
    validation;
  - the "haven't purchased" question marked answered.
- **README.md:**
  - an API section with an example, plus status and layout updates;
  - further improvements: pagination, optimistic locking, segment-aware SMS limits, and the
    shared error framework.
- **PHASE_REVIEWS.md:** a Phase 3 entry.

   - create, list, get, update and delete a campaign;
   - a 422 with several problems;
   - a 409 after setting a campaign's status to `scheduled` in psql;
   - confirm the seeded data is untouched.
````

**Edit** `<repo>/DECISIONS.md`

```
## 19. Campaigns: a minimal lifecycle and a draft-only API

**Context.** Marketers write campaigns by hand now and with AI later. Nothing can schedule or
send a campaign yet.

**Decision.**

- **Lifecycle.** A campaign is `draft`, `scheduled` or `sending`. The allowed moves are:
  - draft → scheduled (approved with a send time);
  - draft → sending (approved to send now);
  - scheduled → draft (unscheduled, to edit again);
  - scheduled → sending (its time arrived).
… [3,920 more characters]
```

```
- [ ] **A separate migration job in production,** with a DDL role, so the API can run with a
      DML-only role ([DECISIONS.md](DECISIONS.md) §6).
- [ ] **Segment-aware SMS limits.** Count GSM-7 and UCS-2 message segments instead of 160
      characters: Turkish letters force UCS-2, which fits 70 characters per message.
- [ ] **Optimistic locking for campaign drafts.** Concurrent edits are last-write-wins today.
- [ ] **Pagination for the campaign list.**
- [ ] **A shared error framework** with stable codes, translations and HTTP mapping at the API
      boundary. Error bodies are local to each capability's handlers today.
```

```
- Apply DDD where invariants exist (e.g. the campaign lifecycle); keep those rules in domain types and methods. Plain CRUD stays plain.
- HTTP handlers stay thin: decode with `httpserver.DecodeJSON`, call the capability's `Service`, answer with `httpserver.WriteJSON`. Rules live in the domain, so other callers (e.g. AI proposals) share them.
```

I've implemented and verified Phase 3; nothing is committed yet. The full review is the new entry at the top of [PHASE_REVIEWS.md](PHASE_REVIEWS.md).

**What was built**
- **Domain** ([internal/campaign](internal/campaign/)):
  - **Lifecycle:** draft, scheduled and sending, with four allowed moves. Only drafts can be updated or deleted.
  - **Content:** an SMS block, an email block or both.
  - **Validation:** every problem is reported at once, each with a field path and a stable code. The checks cover SMS ≤ 160 characters, subject ≤ 80, the three allowed placeholders and malformed braces.
  - **Segment rules:** stored in the typed JSON format: 8 condition types plus nested groups, any of which can be negated, with strict parameters and size limits.
- **One service:** create, update, get, list and delete all go through it, so AI proposals can later reuse it unchanged.
- **Storage:** a `campaigns` table (migration 4) with CHECK constraints. Updates and deletes only apply while the row is still a draft.
- **REST API** under `/api/campaigns`. There are no send or schedule endpoints, and unknown routes get JSON 404/405.
- **Small refactors:** shared `DecodeJSON`/`WriteJSON` helpers (health uses them too), and exported `customer.Channel.Valid()` and `customer.Language.Valid()`.

**Verification**
- `make check` and `make test-integration` pass: 74 unit cases, 6 store cases and 8 new constraint cases. Campaign coverage is 91.7% with PostgreSQL.
- Through nginx with curl:
  - create, get and update succeed (201, 200, 200);
  - an invalid draft gets 422 listing all 4 problems;
  - a campaign set to scheduled in psql can't be changed (409);
  - delete gives 204, then 404;
  - the 5,000 seeded customers are untouched.

**Beyond the plan**
- **JSON for unknown routes:** I added JSON 404/405 bodies after curl showed chi's plain-text defaults.
- **One error type:** validation returns a single error that holds the list of problems.
- **No separate request types:** `Draft` and `Segment` carry JSON tags and double as the request body. Changing those tags is therefore an API change.

**Worth a closer look**
- **Last write wins:** if two people edit the same draft, the later save overwrites the earlier one.
- **SMS length:** messages with Turkish letters fit only 70 characters per SMS in practice. Segment-aware counting is on the README improvements list.
- **City and category names aren't checked yet:** a misspelling like "Istanbul" saves fine until the segmentation phase adds that check.

**Proposed commits** (nothing was pre-staged)
1. `refactor: share JSON helpers and customer channel and language checks` (6 files)
2. `feat(campaign): add campaign domain with segment DSL and content validation` (7 files)
3. `feat(campaign): store campaigns in PostgreSQL behind the campaign service` (5 files: service, store, the migration and its tests)
4. `feat(campaign): add REST API for campaign drafts` (3 files: handlers, their tests and wiring in `cmd/api`)
5. `docs: record phase 3 campaign decisions and review` (4 files)

Reply "commit" for these five, or "single commit" for one `feat: campaign management (phase 3)`.

### Claude

You're right about `bounds`: its `switch` stops at the first problem. With `min: -1, max: -2`, only `min` was reported, which breaks the "every problem at once" rule. While checking, I found the same early stop in two more places:

- **`list`:** after `too_many` it never checked the individual values.
- **`checkPlaceholders`:** a stray `}}` or an empty `{{ }}` ended the scan, so a later `{{coupon}}` went unreported.

On `validName`: it already returned false for space-only names. `strings.TrimSpace("   ")` is `""`, which isn't equal to `"   "`. But that was implicit and untested, so I'll make the check explicit and add test cases.

Fixing all four:

```
// validName accepts a city or category name: not empty or whitespace only, no surrounding
// whitespace, at most 100 characters. Whether it exists is checked when the segment is
// compiled.
func validName(s string) bool {
	trimmed := strings.TrimSpace(s)
	return trimmed != "" && trimmed == s && utf8.RuneCountInString(s) <= maxValueLength
}
```

**Edit** `<repo>/PHASE_REVIEWS.md`

```
- **Tests use `new(30)`,** which Go 1.26 allows for pointers to values.
- **Review fix: complete problem lists.** Three checks used to stop at their first problem:
  - range bounds;
  - lists with more than 50 values;
  - placeholder scanning after a stray `}}` or an empty `{{ }}`.

  They now report every problem, with malformed braces reported once per field.
- **Review fix: names.** `validName` now rejects whitespace-only names explicitly; it already
  did so implicitly. Tests cover spaces, tabs and non-breaking spaces.
```
